Feature: File operation routes
  Scenario: The owner lists a folder by its logical-path prefix
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner lists the folder "photos"
   ✔  Then the listing should name "photos/a.jpg" and "photos/b.jpg"
   ✔  And no answer should carry a filesystem path or a storage key
  Scenario: An upload creates the file's own object in exactly one OCFL version
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner uploads a new file named "c.jpg"
   ✔  Then the upload should create the file's own object at its first version
   ✔  And the object should have exactly 0 new versions
   ✔  And the activity journal should record one "files.upload" entry with opaque identifiers only
  Scenario: A move and a restore each publish one version
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner moves "photos" to "pictures"
   ✔  And the owner restores "photos" from version v1
   ✔  Then the object should have exactly two new versions
   ✔  And "photos/a.jpg" and "pictures/a.jpg" should both be readable
  Scenario: Users see and download a file under its original name
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  Given the owner's original name of "photos/a.jpg" is "Été à Nice 2023.jpg"
   ✔  When the owner lists the folder "photos"
   ✔  And the owner downloads "photos/a.jpg"
   ✔  Then the listing should show "photos/a.jpg" as "Été à Nice 2023.jpg"
   ✔  And the download should be named "Été à Nice 2023.jpg" with an ASCII fallback
  Scenario: A new empty folder stays pending until a file is added beneath it
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner creates the folder "albums"
   ✔  Then the folder should be pending and no version should be published
   ✔  When a file "albums/first.jpg" is committed beneath it
   ✔  Then the folder "albums" should be listed
  Scenario: Folder listings page with an opaque cursor
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner lists the root one entry at a time
   ✔  Then every entry should be listed once, folders first
  Scenario: A ranged read is capped at 16 MiB
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  Given the object holds a 20 MiB file "big.bin"
   ✔  When the owner reads "big.bin" without a range
   ✔  Then the answer should be 206 with the first 16 MiB
  Scenario: A write that changes nothing is refused without a version
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner restores "notes.txt" from version v1 without a change
   ✔  Then the write should be refused as "conflict"
   ✔  And no version should be published
   ✔  And the activity journal should record one refused "files.restore" entry
  Scenario: A client without the token cannot read or write files
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When a client without a token reads "notes.txt"
   ✔  And a client without a token removes "notes.txt"
   ✔  Then both calls should be refused as "unauthorized"
   ✔  And the activity journal should record both denials as missing credentials without the file's path
   ✔  And no version should be published

  Scenario: A server describes itself with its operator's name to any signed-in caller
   ✔  Given a FileSquid server host whose operator name is set to "Garderen Family"

   ✔  Then the descriptor should say host type "server" with operator name "Garderen Family"
  Scenario: Backup and sync are reported separately once enrolled

   ✔  When the owner's webview reads the host descriptor with the per-launch token
   ✔  Then the hosted service should be enrolled at "backup.filesquid.tech"
   ✔  And backup should be reported on and sync off, as two separate values
  Scenario: The host type never follows the request
   ✔  Given a FileSquid desktop host that describes itself to the API
   ✔  When a caller reads the host descriptor with headers that claim a server origin and a browser user agent
   ✔  Then the descriptor should still say host type "desktop"
  Scenario: The operator name is trimmed
   ✔  Given a FileSquid server host whose operator name is set to "  Garderen Family  "
   ✔  When a signed-in member who is not an administrator reads the host descriptor
   ✔  Then the descriptor should say host type "server" with operator name "Garderen Family"
  Scenario: A descriptor change reaches every subscriber
   ✔  Given a FileSquid desktop host that describes itself to the API
   ✔  And an owner's webview and a second authenticated client subscribed to the event channel
   ✔  When the host enrols with the hosted service
   ✔  And the host then turns sync on
   ✔  Then each subscriber should receive two "host.change" events in that order
   ✔  And the first should carry the enrolled descriptor with sync off and the second the descriptor with sync on
  Scenario: A client that missed events reads the descriptor again
   ✔  Given a subscriber that received an "events.resync" hint
   ✔  When it recovers its state
   ✔  Then it should read the host descriptor through the request route
  Scenario: A caller without a credential is refused

   ✔  When a client reads the host descriptor without a credential
   ✔  Then it should be refused as "unauthorized" because of "missing_credentials"
   ✔  And the activity journal should record one "api.auth.denied" entry
  Scenario: The descriptor and its event carry nothing private

   ✔  When the descriptor and a "host.change" payload are inspected
   ✔  Then neither should contain a token, content handle, key or key identifier, account or device identifier, e-mail address, quota, object count, size, timestamp, path or file name

  Scenario: A folder that already holds a file is refused once

   ✔  When the owner creates a library in that folder through the API
   ✔  Then the answer should be the error "conflict"
   ✔  And exactly one refusal "root_not_empty" should be journaled for "library.root.create"

  Scenario: Copy a completed snapshot and rebuild from files through the API
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the owner copies a completed snapshot of the library to an empty folder through the API
   ✔  Then the copy should report 2 objects and no invalid object

   ✔  When the owner rebuilds the library from its files through the API
   ✔  Then the rebuild should flag only the object without a version inventory
  Scenario: The candidate query offers only the object without a version inventory
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the owner asks the API which objects need a version-inventory repair
   ✔  Then only the object without a version inventory should be offered
  Scenario: A consented repair by the desktop owner makes the object committable
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the owner repairs the object without a version inventory with consent through the API
   ✔  Then the object should be answered as repaired
   ✔  And the consent and one outcome row per object should be recorded as "ocfl.repair.version_inventory"
   ✔  And the repaired object should accept a new version
  Scenario: A head that already has both files is refused with nothing_to_repair
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the owner repairs the complete object with consent through the API
   ✔  Then the object should be answered as refused with "nothing_to_repair"
   ✔  And the consent and one outcome row per object should be recorded as "ocfl.repair.version_inventory"
   ✔  And the object's outcome row should be failed with "nothing_to_repair"
   ✔  And the library folder should be unchanged
  Scenario: A repair without consent is refused once and changes nothing
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the owner asks the API to repair the object without a version inventory without consent
   ✔  Then the answer should be the error "conflict"
   ✔  And exactly one refusal "consent_required" should be journaled for "ocfl.repair.version_inventory"
   ✔  And the library folder should be unchanged
  Scenario: A repair without a credential is refused once
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When a caller without a credential asks the API to repair the object without a version inventory
   ✔  Then the answer should be the error "unauthorized"
   ✔  And exactly one refusal "missing_credentials" should be journaled for "api.auth.denied"
   ✔  And the library folder should be unchanged
  Scenario: The server administrator is refused the repair
   ✔  Given the server administrator's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the server administrator repairs the object without a version inventory with consent through the API
   ✔  Then the answer should be the error "forbidden"
   ✔  And exactly one refusal "not_permitted" should be journaled for "api.auth.denied"
   ✔  And the library folder should be unchanged
  Scenario: The server administrator can still rebuild a library
   ✔  Given the server administrator's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the server administrator rebuilds the library from its files through the API
   ✔  Then the rebuild should flag only the object without a version inventory

   ✔  And the unfiled listing should show 2 files
  Scenario: Removing a file from a virtual collection keeps its other memberships
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  And the file is a member of the virtual collections "Best" and "Family"
   ✔  When the owner removes the file from "Best"
   ✔  Then the collection "Trips" should still list the file
   ✔  And the virtual collection "Family" should still list the file
   ✔  And the virtual collection "Best" should list no file
  Scenario: A rule-based virtual collection refuses hand-picked members
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  And a rule-based virtual collection "Recent"
   ✔  When the owner adds the file to "Recent"
   ✔  Then the command should be refused as "invalid_request"
  Scenario: Hidden and deleted files leave the ordinary listing
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 3 files
   ✔  When the owner hides the first file and deletes the second
   ✔  Then the ordinary listing should show 1 file
   ✔  And the hidden view should show 1 file
   ✔  And the deleted view should show 1 file to be purged 30 days after deletion
  Scenario: Permanent deletion needs a confirmation of the exact scope
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 2 files
   ✔  And the owner has deleted every file
   ✔  When the owner permanently deletes every file confirming 1 file
   ✔  Then the command should be refused as "invalid_request"
   ✔  And the deleted view should show 2 files
  Scenario: A command against a stale revision is a conflict and changes nothing
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  When the owner renames the collection "Trips" with a stale revision
   ✔  Then the command should be refused as "conflict"
   ✔  And nothing should have been committed
  Scenario: Retrying a command with the same operation ID changes nothing
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  When the owner sends the same create-collection command twice
   ✔  Then both answers should name the same collection
   ✔  And the library should list 2 collections
  Scenario: A command that changes nothing stores nothing
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  When the owner renames the collection "Trips" to "Trips" as operation "op-same-title"
   ✔  Then the command should answer committed at the current revision
   ✔  And the catalog should hold no receipt for operation "op-same-title"
   ✔  And nothing should have been committed
   ✔  And no journal entry should have been stored
  Scenario: A retried command that changed nothing is planned again
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  And the owner has renamed the collection "Trips" to "Trips" as operation "op-same-title"
   ✔  When the owner renames the collection "Trips" to "Holidays"
   ✔  And the owner retries operation "op-same-title"
   ✔  Then the command should be refused as "conflict"
  Scenario: Paging visits every file once in a stable order
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 7 files
   ✔  When the owner lists the library 3 files per page
   ✔  Then every file should appear exactly once in identifier order
   ✔  And a query asking for 1001 items per page should be refused
  Scenario: The journal records every change without names or titles
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Holiday in Lisbon" holding 1 file
   ✔  When the owner renames the collection "Holiday in Lisbon" to "Secret plans"
   ✔  Then the catalog should hold one journal entry per commit
   ✔  And no journal entry should contain "Lisbon" or "Secret plans"
Feature: Local library create, open, copy, rebuild and version-inventory repair
  Scenario: Create a library in an empty folder without an account or a connection
   ✔  Given an empty folder chosen by the owner
   ✔  When the owner creates a library there
   ✔  Then the folder should hold only the OCFL 1.1 storage-root declaration
   ✔  And the creation should be journaled as succeeded
  Scenario: A folder that already holds files is refused
   ✔  Given a folder chosen by the owner that already holds a file
   ✔  When the owner creates a library there
   ✔  Then the request should be refused with "root_not_empty"
   ✔  And nothing should be written to the folder
  Scenario: Copying a completed snapshot leaves the library untouched
   ✔  Given a library with a complete object and an object whose head has no version inventory
   ✔  When the owner copies a completed snapshot to an empty folder
   ✔  Then the copy should validate without errors and open as a library of 2 objects
   ✔  And nothing should be written to the library
   ✔  And the object without a version inventory should still lack it
  Scenario: Rebuilding from files reports problems and repairs nothing
   ✔  Given a library with a complete object and an object whose head has no version inventory
   ✔  When the owner rebuilds the library from its files
   ✔  Then the rebuild should list 2 objects
   ✔  And the object without a version inventory should be flagged as a repair candidate
   ✔  And nothing should be written to the library
  Scenario: The candidate query lists the objects to offer
   ✔  Given a library with a complete object and an object whose head has no version inventory
   ✔  When the owner asks which objects need a version-inventory repair
   ✔  Then only the object without a version inventory should be listed
  Scenario: A consented repair by the owner makes the object committable
   ✔  Given a library with an object whose head has no version inventory
   ✔  And a new version of that object is refused until it is repaired
   ✔  When the owner consents and repairs that object
   ✔  Then the object should be repaired
   ✔  And a new version of that object should be accepted
   ✔  And the consent and one outcome per object should be journaled without paths
  Scenario: The repair is refused without consent
   ✔  Given a library with an object whose head has no version inventory
   ✔  When the repair is requested without the owner's consent
   ✔  Then the request should be refused with "consent_required"
   ✔  And nothing should be written to the library
   ✔  And the refusal should be journaled
  Scenario: The repair is refused for another caller
   ✔  Given a library with an object whose head has no version inventory
   ✔  When the server administrator requests the repair with consent
   ✔  Then the request should be refused with "forbidden"
   ✔  And nothing should be written to the library
   ✔  And the refusal should be journaled
  Scenario: A head that already has its version inventory is not repaired again
   ✔  Given a library with a complete object
   ✔  When the owner consents and repairs that object
   ✔  Then the object should be refused with "nothing_to_repair"
   ✔  And nothing should be written to the library
Feature: Metadata property routes
  Scenario: An upload creates the file's object with its original and metadata record
   ✔  When the owner uploads "Holiday 2023.JPG" as a new file
   ✔  Then the file's object should hold the original and its metadata record in version "v1"
   ✔  And the record should name "Holiday 2023.JPG" with the client's facts and the receiving host's system, architecture and FileSquid version
   ✔  And every source, volume and attribute fact the upload did not supply should be listed as unavailable
  Scenario: An upload whose declared size differs commits nothing
   ✔  When the owner uploads "a.txt" declaring 9 bytes but sending 5
   ✔  Then the metadata answer should be the error "invalid_request"
   ✔  And no file object should have been created
  Scenario: Statements are set, read and removed, one version each
   ✔  Given the owner uploaded "harbour.jpg"
   ✔  When the owner sets "dcterms:title" to "Harbour"
   ✔  And the owner sets "dc:creator" to "Ana"
   ✔  And the owner removes "dcterms:creator"
   ✔  Then the file should show only "dcterms:title" as "Harbour"
   ✔  And the file's object should be at version "v4"

   ✔  When the owner sets "dcterms:colour" to "red"
   ✔  Then the metadata answer should be the error "invalid_request"
   ✔  When the owner sets "rico:title" to "Fonds"
   ✔  Then the metadata answer should be the error "invalid_request"
   ✔  And the file's object should be at version "v1"
  Scenario: A registered namespace accepts its own terms
   ✔  Given the owner uploaded "a.txt"
   ✔  When the owner registers the namespace "rico" with the term "title"
   ✔  And the owner sets "rico:title" to "Fonds"
   ✔  Then the file should show only "rico:title" as "Fonds"
  Scenario: A bare term name is offered Dublin Core first
   ✔  Given the owner registered the namespace "rico" with the term "title"
   ✔  When the owner asks where "title" can go
   ✔  Then the answer should offer "dcterms:title", "schema:title" and "rico:title" in that order
  Scenario: The import record's facts show no absolute path, host or account
   ✔  Given the owner uploaded "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner reads the file's facts
   ✔  Then the facts should name "notes.txt" with its size and method "api_upload"
   ✔  And the facts should hold no absolute path, import root, host or account name
  Scenario: Content answers use the record's display name and media type
   ✔  Given the owner uploaded "Café.md" as "text/markdown"
   ✔  When the owner reads the file's original
   ✔  Then the answer should carry the media type "text/markdown" and the name "Café.md"
Feature: Navigation listing
  Scenario: The root level is the host's repository

   ✔  Then the answer should hold exactly one node of kind "repository"

Feature: OCFL validation findings in health and the Monitor
  Scenario: A stray file under vN/ degrades health until it is removed
   ✔  Given an open library with a valid object and an object with a stray file under its head version
   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  Then health should be "degraded" with reason "ocfl_validation_errors" naming "E023" in 1 object
   ✔  And one "ocfl.validation.finding" entry for "E023" should be journaled without paths
   ✔  When the stray file is removed and the library is validated again
   ✔  Then one "ocfl.validation.finding_closed" entry for "E023" should be journaled without paths
   ✔  And health should be "ok"
  Scenario: An object without a version inventory is a warning only
   ✔  Given an open library with an object whose head has no version inventory
   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  Then the findings should list "W010" as a warning
   ✔  And one "ocfl.validation.finding" entry for "W010" should be journaled without paths
   ✔  And health should be "ok"
  Scenario: The takeover check validates the object a takeover commit wrote
   ✔  Given an open library with a valid object and an object with a stray file under its head version
   ✔  When the takeover check runs for the object with the stray file after a commit that took over an expired reservation
   ✔  Then health should be "degraded" with reason "ocfl_validation_errors" naming "E023" in 1 object
  Scenario: The administrator sees findings without locations
   ✔  Given an open library with a valid object and an object with a stray file under its head version
   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  Then the owner should see where the "E023" finding is
   ✔  And the server administrator should see the "E023" finding without its location
  Scenario: A capped run reports what it did not check and the next run resumes

   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  Then the run should be "capped" with 2 objects checked and 1 not checked
   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  Then the cursor should have wrapped to the first object
  Scenario: Deleting an object closes its findings
   ✔  Given an open library with a valid object and an object with a stray file under its head version
   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  And the object with the stray file is deleted and the library is validated again
   ✔  Then one "ocfl.validation.finding_closed" entry for "E023" should be journaled without paths
   ✔  And health should be "ok"

Feature: PROV-O records, history routes and backfill in filesquid-api
  Scenario: A repaired object gets a provenance record under the repair run ID
   ✔  Given a library with an object whose head has no version inventory, recording provenance
   ✔  When the owner repairs that object
   ✔  Then a new version of the object should hold a PROV-O record of kind "repair"
   ✔  And the record, the consent and the outcome should share the repair run ID
   ✔  And the new version should carry its "ocfl.commit" journal entry under the ID the journal stored
  Scenario: Without a provenance context the repair says that no record was written
   ✔  Given a library with an object whose head has no version inventory, without provenance
   ✔  When the owner repairs that object
   ✔  Then the repair answer should name the provenance error "provenance_not_configured"
   ✔  And a "prov.record" warning should be journaled under the repair run ID
  Scenario: A backfill reconstructs every missing record once
   ✔  Given a library of 2 objects committed before the history existed
   ✔  When the history backfill runs
   ✔  Then every version should have a record and the earlier ones should be marked reconstructed
   ✔  And the backfill process should end "completed" with 2 of 2 objects done
   ✔  When the history backfill runs again
   ✔  Then the second run should commit nothing
  Scenario: A backfill stops at its cap and the next run continues
   ✔  Given a library of 2 objects committed before the history existed
   ✔  When the history backfill runs with a cap of 3 records
   ✔  Then the run should report that it reached its cap
   ✔  When the history backfill runs again
   ✔  Then every version should have a record and the earlier ones should be marked reconstructed
  Scenario: The history of a file lists its activities in commit order
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller lists the history of the first file
   ✔  Then the answer should hold 2 entries of that file, oldest first
  Scenario: The history is paged with an opaque cursor
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller lists the whole history 2 entries at a time
   ✔  Then the pages should hold 2 and then 1 entries and the last page no cursor
  Scenario: The record of one version is raw JSON-LD
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller reads the record of version "v2" of the first file
   ✔  Then the answer should be the JSON-LD record of that version
  Scenario: A version without a record is not found
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller reads the record of version "v9" of the first file
   ✔  Then the response should be an "not_found" error with a message
  Scenario: Two filter dimensions at once are refused
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller lists the history by object and by operation at once
   ✔  Then the response should be an "invalid_request" error with a message
  Scenario: A redacted export names no installation and no path
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller exports the history of the first file redacted
   ✔  Then the export should be a JSON-LD array of 2 records without the installation or any path
  Scenario: The history routes wait for the host to install its history
   ✔  Given a host that has not installed its history yet
   ✔  When the caller lists the history of the first file
   ✔  Then the response should be an "unavailable" error with a message
   ✔  And the error should be retryable
Feature: Replica, cache identity and pending changes
  Scenario: A stored view answers with its coverage
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given the replica stored a complete view for alice
   ✔  When alice reads the view from the replica
   ✔  Then the answer should say the coverage is complete
  Scenario: An incomplete view says so
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given the replica stored a view with 3 of 8 index parts loaded
   ✔  When alice reads the view from the replica
   ✔  Then the answer should say 3 of 8 parts are loaded
  Scenario: Another viewer never reads alice's stored view
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given the replica stored a complete view for alice
   ✔  When bob reads the same view from the replica
   ✔  Then the replica should report "not_cached"
  Scenario: A changed grant makes the old stored view unreachable
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given the replica stored a complete view for alice
   ✔  When alice's grants shrink to "file.read" and she reads the same view
   ✔  Then the replica should report "not_cached"
  Scenario: A stored view made for another identity is refused
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given the replica holds a view made for bob under alice's key
   ✔  When alice reads the view from the replica
   ✔  Then the replica should report "identity_mismatch"
  Scenario: An evicted view says it was evicted
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given the replica stored a complete view for alice
   ✔  And the device evicts that view
   ✔  When alice reads the view from the replica
   ✔  Then the replica should report "evicted"
   ✔  And the answer should not be an empty page
  Scenario: A new change is pending, not canonical
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  When alice hides a file while offline
   ✔  Then the change should be "queued"
   ✔  And library "family" should have 1 pending change
  Scenario: A change becomes canonical only after its fenced head publication
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given alice has queued a change
   ✔  When the change begins publishing and the head publication succeeds at revision "4"
   ✔  Then the change should be "committed"
   ✔  And library "family" should have 0 pending changes
  Scenario: A lost race is a conflict that blocks later changes
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given alice has queued two changes
   ✔  When the first change begins publishing and loses the race
   ✔  Then the first change should be "conflict"
   ✔  And the second change should not be allowed to publish
  Scenario: Repeated failures stall a change for the owner to decide
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given alice has queued a change
   ✔  When the change fails to publish 5 times in a row
   ✔  Then the change should be "stalled"
  Scenario: Pending changes survive a reload
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given alice has queued a change
   ✔  When the device reloads from the saved list of pending changes
   ✔  Then the change should be "queued"
  Scenario: A damaged saved list is refused
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  When the device reloads from a damaged saved list
   ✔  Then the saved list should be refused as "invalid_snapshot"

   ✔  And the library's history should keep 2 contents in 11 bytes

   ✔  Then the contract should offer no version deletion, expiry or thinning

     Running tests/features.rs (<path>)
Feature: Every fsquid command that changes something or fails is journaled
  Scenario: A command that commits is journaled once with opaque IDs only
dcterms:title is a valid term
fsquid metadata — metadata CRUD (via filesquid-ocfl)

Usage:
  fsquid <command> [args] [--object <dir>] [--id <object-id>]
  fsquid metadata <command> [args] [--object <dir>] [--id <object-id>]

Commands:
  extract <file>          Extract EXIF/ID3/XMP from a media file into metadata.md
  extract --file <logical-path>
                          Extract from a logical file of the object
  get [prefix:term]       Print populated statements (or one CURIE)
  set <prefix:term> <val> Set a statement and commit metadata.md
  export csv|xmp|json <path>  Write CSV, XMP or JSON sidecar interchange
  import csv|xmp|json <path>  Read CSV, XMP or JSON sidecar into metadata.md
  validate [prefix:term]  Check a term against the /terms/ register and the
                          namespace registry, or, without a term, every
                          statement of metadata.md; names each unregistered
                          prefix (always run as `fsquid metadata validate`)
  namespace register <prefix> <iri> [--terms a,b,...] [--custom]
                          Register an extension namespace (optional term
                          list) or, with --custom, declare a custom namespace
                          of free keys
  namespace list          Print the registered namespaces
  help                    Show this help

--object is an OCFL object directory (default `.`). --id names the object:
required to create one in a directory without an object, and checked
against an existing object's identifier.

--registry is the OCFL object directory that keeps the namespace registry
(namespaces.md); without it, the registry is the one in --object.
dcterms:, dc:, schema: and exif: are built in and cannot be registered.

JSON sidecars also take --file <logical-path>: the logical file of the
object's head whose SHA-256 digest binds the sidecar with the object
identifier. Import rejects a sidecar whose identifier or digest differs.

Canonical store: metadata.md (YAML front matter + Markdown body), a logical
file of the OCFL object. Each edit commits a new OCFL version; earlier
versions are never modified.
   ✔> Given a fresh FileSquid data directory
   ✔  When I set a title on a new object with fsquid
   ✔  Then the journal should hold one "cli.metadata.set" entry that "succeeded"
   ✔  And that entry should name the object and its version but no path or value
  Scenario: A command that changes nothing is not journaled
   ✔> Given a fresh FileSquid data directory
   ✔  When I set a title on a new object with fsquid
   ✔  And I set the same title again with fsquid
   ✔  And I read the title with fsquid
   ✔  Then the journal should hold 1 entry
  Scenario: A successful export writes a file outside the object and is journaled
   ✔> Given a fresh FileSquid data directory
   ✔  When I set a title on a new object with fsquid
   ✔  And I export the object's metadata as CSV with fsquid
   ✔  Then the journal should hold 2 entries
   ✔  And the last entry should be a "cli.metadata.export" entry that "succeeded"
  Scenario: fsquid writes beside a running desktop host
   ✔> Given a fresh FileSquid data directory
   ✔  Given another connection holds the journal open
   ✔  When I set a title on a new object with fsquid
   ✔  Then that connection should see the "cli.metadata.set" entry
  Scenario: A command line that names no module command is journaled as a failed route
   ✔> Given a fresh FileSquid data directory
   ✔  When I run the fsquid binary with "validate dcterms:title"
   ✔  Then the journal should hold one "cli.route" entry that "failed" with "module_required"
  Scenario: A journal that cannot be opened refuses the command
   ✔> Given a fresh FileSquid data directory
   ✔  Given a data directory that cannot hold a journal
   ✔  When I set a title on a new object with fsquid
   ✔  Then fsquid should refuse with one journal_unavailable line naming "cli.metadata.set"
   ✔  And no object should have been written
Feature: One command-line tool named fsquid

Feature: C2PA and PROV-O record types
  Scenario: An import record is bound to resource identity and SHA-256
   ✔  Given a file resource "res-1" with digest "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef"
   ✔  When I initialize a C2PA import record at "2026-09-23T12:00:00Z"
   ✔  Then the import record resource should be "res-1"
   ✔  And the import digest should be "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef"
   ✔  And the import digest algorithm should be "sha-256"
   ✔  And the import time should be "2026-09-23T12:00:00Z"
   ✔  And the validation status should be "unsigned"
  Scenario: A prepared unsigned record is not a valid signed manifest
   ✔  Given a file resource "res-1" with digest "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef"

   ✔  And the record should be a valid signed manifest
   ✔  When I set validation status to "invalid"
   ✔  Then the validation status should be "invalid"

     Running tests/features.rs (<path>)
Feature: Reusable filesquid-metadata engine
  Scenario: Core API is namespaced metadata
fsquid metadata — metadata CRUD (via filesquid-ocfl)

Usage:
  fsquid <command> [args] [--object <dir>] [--id <object-id>]
  fsquid metadata <command> [args] [--object <dir>] [--id <object-id>]

Commands:
  extract <file>          Extract EXIF/ID3/XMP from a media file into metadata.md
  extract --file <logical-path>
                          Extract from a logical file of the object
  get [prefix:term]       Print populated statements (or one CURIE)
  set <prefix:term> <val> Set a statement and commit metadata.md
  export csv|xmp|json <path>  Write CSV, XMP or JSON sidecar interchange
  import csv|xmp|json <path>  Read CSV, XMP or JSON sidecar into metadata.md
  validate [prefix:term]  Check a term against the /terms/ register and the
                          namespace registry, or, without a term, every
                          statement of metadata.md; names each unregistered
                          prefix (always run as `fsquid metadata validate`)
  namespace register <prefix> <iri> [--terms a,b,...] [--custom]
                          Register an extension namespace (optional term
                          list) or, with --custom, declare a custom namespace
                          of free keys
  namespace list          Print the registered namespaces
  help                    Show this help

--object is an OCFL object directory (default `.`). --id names the object:
required to create one in a directory without an object, and checked
against an existing object's identifier.

--registry is the OCFL object directory that keeps the namespace registry
(namespaces.md); without it, the registry is the one in --object.
dcterms:, dc:, schema: and exif: are built in and cannot be registered.

JSON sidecars also take --file <logical-path>: the logical file of the
object's head whose SHA-256 digest binds the sidecar with the object
identifier. Import rejects a sidecar whose identifier or digest differs.

Canonical store: metadata.md (YAML front matter + Markdown body), a logical
file of the OCFL object. Each edit commits a new OCFL version; earlier
versions are never modified.
   ✔  When I inspect the filesquid-metadata public API

Feature: Intent, OCFL snapshot commit and receipt reconcile
  Scenario: A change is published as an OCFL version before it is acknowledged
   ✔  Given an empty catalog directory
   ✔  When the owner creates the library "Family archive" with the collection "Summer"
   ✔  Then the library's OCFL snapshot should be at version "v2"
   ✔  And no intent should be pending
  Scenario: A crash before the snapshot leaves the work pending
   ✔  Given an empty catalog directory whose commits crash before the snapshot of "op-summer"
   ✔  And the owner created the library "Family archive"
   ✔  When the owner adds the collection "Summer" under operation "op-summer"
   ✔  And the host restarts
   ✔  Then the intent of "op-summer" should be pending
   ✔  And the library's OCFL snapshot should be at version "v1"
   ✔  When the owner adds the collection "Summer" under operation "op-summer"
   ✔  Then the library's OCFL snapshot should be at version "v2"
   ✔  And no intent should be pending
  Scenario: A crash after the snapshot loses no acknowledged state
   ✔  Given an empty catalog directory whose commits crash after the snapshot of "op-summer"

   ✔  And the host restarts
   ✔  Then the collection "Summer" should be listed
   ✔  And no intent should be pending
   ✔  And the library's OCFL snapshot should be at version "v2"

   ✔  And replaying "op-summer" should answer its first result without a new version

   ✔  And every version should read back as its plaintext source

   ✔  And every version should read back as its plaintext source

   ✔  When I stream every logical file of every version in chunks of 2 bytes

Feature: Version-bound logical files
  Scenario: A commit carries a record named after its reserved version
   ✔> Given an object committed at "v1" with "a.txt", "b.txt" and "c.txt" through the change model
   ✔  Given writer "writer-a" staged new bytes for "a.txt"
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits the staged changes with a version-bound record
   ✔  Then the object head should be "v2" through the change model
   ✔  And "v2" should hold the record "record/v2.txt" naming "v2" and its created time
  Scenario: A record-only commit adds one version holding only the record
   ✔> Given an object committed at "v1" with "a.txt", "b.txt" and "c.txt" through the change model
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits only a version-bound record
   ✔  Then the object head should be "v2" through the change model
   ✔  And "v2" should hold the record "record/v2.txt" naming "v2" and its created time
  Scenario: A rebased commit binds its record to the new version
   ✔> Given an object committed at "v1" with "a.txt", "b.txt" and "c.txt" through the change model
   ✔  Given writer "writer-a" staged new bytes for "a.txt"
   ✔  And writer "writer-b" committed "v2" with new bytes for "b.txt"
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits the staged changes with a version-bound record
   ✔  Then the object head should be "v3" through the change model
   ✔  And "v3" should hold the record "record/v3.txt" naming "v3" and its created time
  Scenario: A failing record function publishes nothing
   ✔> Given an object committed at "v1" with "a.txt", "b.txt" and "c.txt" through the change model
   ✔  Given writer "writer-a" staged new bytes for "a.txt"
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits the staged changes with a failing record function
   ✔  Then the commit should be refused for its version-bound files
   ✔  And the object head should be "v1" through the change model

   ✔  Given a copy of the pinned W010 fixture object

Feature: PROV-O model and profile filesquid-prov/1
  Scenario: One activity describes the committed version
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then the record should hold exactly one prov:Activity named after the operation, object and version
   ✔  And its end time should equal the version's created time
   ✔  And it should name the journal entry IDs of the commit
  Scenario: Added, changed, moved and removed paths become entities
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then the changed path should be generated by the activity and derived from its previous state
   ✔  And the moved path should be derived from the old path's previous state
   ✔  And the removed path's previous state should be invalidated by the activity
   ✔  And every previous state the activity replaced should be used by it
   ✔  And every entity should carry its object ID, version, logical path and SHA-256 digest
  Scenario: The owner is an installation agent, never a person
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then the activity should be associated with "urn:uuid:" followed by the installation UUID
   ✔  And the FileSquid host should be a prov:SoftwareAgent acting on behalf of the installation
   ✔  And the record should contain no prov:Person and no display name
  Scenario: Agents the caller did not observe are stated as unrecorded
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given the caller observed no installation and no host
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then both agents should be stated as unrecorded
  Scenario: Equal facts give equal bytes
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I serialize the record twice from equal facts
   ✔  Then both documents should be byte-for-byte equal
   ✔  And the graph should list the activity first, then agents and entities sorted by identifier
  Scenario: A record survives a parse round trip
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I serialize the record and parse it again
   ✔  Then the parsed record should equal the original
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that is larger than 1 MiB
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_TOO_LARGE"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that nests deeper than 16 levels
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_TOO_DEEP"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that repeats a key
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_DUPLICATE_KEY"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that names an unknown profile
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_UNKNOWN_PROFILE"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound

   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_INVALID_NODE"

  Scenario: The history index pages an object's activities in order

   ✔  When I read the activities of the first object in pages of 2

  Scenario Outline: Page sizes outside 1 to 1000 are refused

   ✔  And the commit should have written nothing
  Scenario: A moved head is rebased onto when no staged path changed
   ✔> Given an object committed at "v1" with "a.txt", "b.txt" and "c.txt" through the change model
   ✔  Given writer "writer-a" staged new bytes for "a.txt"
   ✔  And writer "writer-b" committed "v2" with new bytes for "b.txt"
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits the staged changes
   ✔  Then the object head should be "v3" through the change model
   ✔  And "v3" should hold the changes of both writers
  Scenario: A moved head that changed a staged path is a conflict
   ✔> Given an object committed at "v1" with "a.txt", "b.txt" and "c.txt" through the change model
   ✔  Given writer "writer-a" staged new bytes for "a.txt"
   ✔  And writer "writer-b" committed "v2" with new bytes for "a.txt"
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits the staged changes
   ✔  Then the commit should be refused as a conflict on "a.txt"
   ✔  And the object head should be "v2" through the change model
  Scenario: An empty change set publishes nothing
   ✔> Given an object committed at "v1" with "a.txt", "b.txt" and "c.txt" through the change model
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits the staged changes
   ✔  Then the commit should be refused as nothing to commit
   ✔  And the object head should be "v1" through the change model
  Scenario: A head without its version inventory stays refused and is not repaired
   ✔> Given an object committed at "v1" with "a.txt", "b.txt" and "c.txt" through the change model
   ✔  Given the head version inventory of "v1" was removed
   ✔  And writer "writer-a" staged new bytes for "a.txt"
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits the staged changes
   ✔  Then the commit should be refused for the missing version inventory
   ✔  And the head version inventory of "v1" should still be missing
  Scenario: Staging inside the object root is refused
   ✔> Given an object committed at "v1" with "a.txt", "b.txt" and "c.txt" through the change model
   ✔  When a writer stages a file in a staging area inside the object root
   ✔  Then staging should be refused as inside an OCFL root
Feature: Enumerate committed logical state
  Scenario: Each committed file at head is enumerated with its locator
   ✔> Given an OCFL storage root in memory holding the objects "ab/obj1" with three versions and "cd/obj2" with one
   ✔  When I enumerate the committed logical state at each object's head
   ✔  Then the enumeration should be:
       | event   | object          | version | path     | content locator             |
       | object  | urn:example:one | v3      |          | ab/obj1                     |
       | version | urn:example:one | v3      |          |                             |
       | file    | urn:example:one | v3      | a.txt    | ab/obj1/v3/content/a.txt    |
       | file    | urn:example:one | v3      | c.txt    | ab/obj1/v1/content/b.txt    |
       | object  | urn:example:two | v1      |          | cd/obj2                     |
       | version | urn:example:two | v1      |          |                             |
       | file    | urn:example:two | v1      | only.txt | cd/obj2/v1/content/only.txt |
   ✔  And every enumerated locator should hold bytes that match the file's digest
  Scenario: Every committed version is enumerated oldest first
   ✔> Given an OCFL storage root in memory holding the objects "ab/obj1" with three versions and "cd/obj2" with one
   ✔  When I enumerate the committed logical state of every version
   ✔  Then the versions enumerated for "urn:example:one" should be "v1, v2, v3"
   ✔  And version "v2" of "urn:example:one" should list "a.txt, c.txt, d.txt"
  Scenario: Files the inventory does not list are not enumerated
   ✔> Given an OCFL storage root in memory holding the objects "ab/obj1" with three versions and "cd/obj2" with one
   ✔  Given a stray file "v3/content/stray.txt" in the object "ab/obj1"
   ✔  When I enumerate the committed logical state of every version
   ✔  Then no enumerated file should be "stray.txt"
  Scenario: A version above the committed head is not enumerated
   ✔> Given an OCFL storage root in memory holding the objects "ab/obj1" with three versions and "cd/obj2" with one
   ✔  Given version "v4" content and a version inventory exist in "ab/obj1" while its root head is "v3"

   ✔  Then the versions enumerated for "urn:example:one" should be "v1, v2, v3"
  Scenario: A broken object is reported and the others are still enumerated
   ✔> Given an OCFL storage root in memory holding the objects "ab/obj1" with three versions and "cd/obj2" with one
   ✔  Given the object "b/broken" in the storage root has no root inventory
   ✔  When I enumerate the committed logical state at each object's head
   ✔  Then the enumeration should report "b/broken" with OCFL code "E063"
   ✔  And the objects enumerated should be "ab/obj1, cd/obj2"

   ✔  When an independent reader recovers the copy with only a JSON parser and SHA-2

   ✔> Given an OCFL object "obj" committed at "v1" through the engine writer
   ✔  Given writer "writer-a" crashed while writing "obj/inventory.json.sha256" during the commit of "v2"
   ✔  When I open the written object
   ✔  Then the object head should be "v1"
   ✔  And pinning "v2" should be refused as an unknown version
   ✔  When the crashed writer's lease lapses and writer "writer-b" retries the commit
   ✔  Then the object head should be "v2"
   ✔  And the commit receipt should report a reservation takeover
   ✔  And the history should list "v1, v2"
   ✔  And the object should validate with zero errors
  Scenario: A second writer cannot reserve a version that is held
   ✔> Given an OCFL object "obj" committed at "v1" through the engine writer
   ✔  Given writer "writer-b" holds the reservation for "v2"
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits "v2" adding "d.txt"
   ✔  Then the commit should be refused because the reservation is held
   ✔  And no byte should be written under "v2"
  Scenario: A stale lease holder cannot write content after reassignment
   ✔> Given an OCFL object "obj" committed at "v1" through the engine writer
   ✔  Given the lease passes to writer "writer-b" after "obj/v2/content/d.txt" is written
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits "v2" adding "d.txt" and "e.txt"
   ✔  Then the commit should be refused because the reservation was lost
   ✔  And "obj/v2/content/e.txt" should not be stored
   ✔  And the object head should be "v1"
  Scenario: A stale lease holder cannot publish the head after reassignment
   ✔> Given an OCFL object "obj" committed at "v1" through the engine writer
   ✔  Given the lease passes to writer "writer-b" after "obj/v2/inventory.json.sha256" is written
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits "v2" adding "d.txt"
   ✔  Then the commit should be refused because the reservation was lost
   ✔  And the root inventory should be unchanged
   ✔  And the object head should be "v1"
  Scenario: A moved head is refused without publishing
   ✔> Given an OCFL object "obj" committed at "v1" through the engine writer
   ✔  Given writer "writer-b" has committed "v2" adding "d.txt"
   ✔  When writer "writer-a" commits on top of "v1" adding "e.txt"

   ✔  And the object head should be "v2"

Feature: PROV-O model and profile filesquid-prov/1
  Scenario: One activity describes the committed version
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then the record should hold exactly one prov:Activity named after the operation, object and version
   ✔  And its end time should equal the version's created time
   ✔  And it should name the journal entry IDs of the commit
  Scenario: Added, changed, moved and removed paths become entities
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then the changed path should be generated by the activity and derived from its previous state
   ✔  And the moved path should be derived from the old path's previous state
   ✔  And the removed path's previous state should be invalidated by the activity
   ✔  And every previous state the activity replaced should be used by it
   ✔  And every entity should carry its object ID, version, logical path and SHA-256 digest
  Scenario: The owner is an installation agent, never a person
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then the activity should be associated with "urn:uuid:" followed by the installation UUID
   ✔  And the FileSquid host should be a prov:SoftwareAgent acting on behalf of the installation
   ✔  And the record should contain no prov:Person and no display name
  Scenario: Agents the caller did not observe are stated as unrecorded
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given the caller observed no installation and no host
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then both agents should be stated as unrecorded
  Scenario: Equal facts give equal bytes
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I serialize the record twice from equal facts
   ✔  Then both documents should be byte-for-byte equal
   ✔  And the graph should list the activity first, then agents and entities sorted by identifier
  Scenario: A record survives a parse round trip
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I serialize the record and parse it again
   ✔  Then the parsed record should equal the original
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that is larger than 1 MiB
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_TOO_LARGE"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that nests deeper than 16 levels
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_TOO_DEEP"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that repeats a key
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_DUPLICATE_KEY"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that names an unknown profile
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_UNKNOWN_PROFILE"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that holds an entity with a forged ID
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_INVALID_NODE"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario: The history index pages an object's activities in order
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given an index of 5 activities across 2 objects
   ✔  When I read the activities of the first object in pages of 2
   ✔  Then I should read 3 activities ordered by end time then activity ID
   ✔  And the last page should carry no cursor
  Scenario Outline: Page sizes outside 1 to 1000 are refused
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given an index of 5 activities across 2 objects
   ✔  When I ask for a page of 0 activities
   ✔  Then the page request should be refused
  Scenario Outline: Page sizes outside 1 to 1000 are refused
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given an index of 5 activities across 2 objects
   ✔  When I ask for a page of 1 activities
   ✔  Then the page request should be accepted
  Scenario Outline: Page sizes outside 1 to 1000 are refused
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given an index of 5 activities across 2 objects
   ✔  When I ask for a page of 1000 activities
   ✔  Then the page request should be accepted
  Scenario Outline: Page sizes outside 1 to 1000 are refused
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given an index of 5 activities across 2 objects
   ✔  When I ask for a page of 1001 activities
   ✔  Then the page request should be refused
  Scenario: The module stands alone
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I inspect the crate manifest
   ✔  Then no default dependency should be another FileSquid crate
   ✔  And no feature should name filesquid-native or filesquid-c2pa

   ✔  Then the build should succeed

   ✔  When the owner renames the file to "写真 — 夏"
   ✔  Then the download fallback should be the file identifier plus ".jpg"

   ✔  And the outgoing name under the original-name policy should be "Été à Nice.jpg"

Feature: File operation routes
  Scenario: The owner lists a folder by its logical-path prefix
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner lists the folder "photos"
   ✔  Then the listing should name "photos/a.jpg" and "photos/b.jpg"
   ✔  And no answer should carry a filesystem path or a storage key
  Scenario: An upload creates the file's own object in exactly one OCFL version
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner uploads a new file named "c.jpg"
   ✔  Then the upload should create the file's own object at its first version
   ✔  And the object should have exactly 0 new versions
   ✔  And the activity journal should record one "files.upload" entry with opaque identifiers only
  Scenario: A move and a restore each publish one version
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner moves "photos" to "pictures"
   ✔  And the owner restores "photos" from version v1
   ✔  Then the object should have exactly two new versions
   ✔  And "photos/a.jpg" and "pictures/a.jpg" should both be readable
  Scenario: Users see and download a file under its original name
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  Given the owner's original name of "photos/a.jpg" is "Été à Nice 2023.jpg"
   ✔  When the owner lists the folder "photos"
   ✔  And the owner downloads "photos/a.jpg"
   ✔  Then the listing should show "photos/a.jpg" as "Été à Nice 2023.jpg"
   ✔  And the download should be named "Été à Nice 2023.jpg" with an ASCII fallback
  Scenario: A new empty folder stays pending until a file is added beneath it
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner creates the folder "albums"
   ✔  Then the folder should be pending and no version should be published
   ✔  When a file "albums/first.jpg" is committed beneath it
   ✔  Then the folder "albums" should be listed
  Scenario: Folder listings page with an opaque cursor
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner lists the root one entry at a time
   ✔  Then every entry should be listed once, folders first
  Scenario: A ranged read is capped at 16 MiB
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  Given the object holds a 20 MiB file "big.bin"
   ✔  When the owner reads "big.bin" without a range
   ✔  Then the answer should be 206 with the first 16 MiB
  Scenario: A write that changes nothing is refused without a version
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner restores "notes.txt" from version v1 without a change
   ✔  Then the write should be refused as "conflict"
   ✔  And no version should be published
   ✔  And the activity journal should record one refused "files.restore" entry
  Scenario: A client without the token cannot read or write files
   ✔> Given the owner's library holds an object with "photos/a.jpg", "photos/b.jpg" and "notes.txt"
   ✔  When a client without a token reads "notes.txt"
   ✔  And a client without a token removes "notes.txt"
   ✔  Then both calls should be refused as "unauthorized"
   ✔  And the activity journal should record both denials as missing credentials without the file's path
   ✔  And no version should be published

Feature: Host descriptor
  Scenario: The desktop host describes itself to the owner's webview
   ✔  Given a FileSquid desktop host that describes itself to the API
   ✔  When the owner's webview reads the host descriptor with the per-launch token
   ✔  Then the descriptor should say host type "desktop" with no operator name
   ✔  And the hosted service should be reported as not enrolled
  Scenario: A server describes itself with its operator's name to any signed-in caller
   ✔  Given a FileSquid server host whose operator name is set to "Garderen Family"
   ✔  When a signed-in member who is not an administrator reads the host descriptor
   ✔  Then the descriptor should say host type "server" with operator name "Garderen Family"
  Scenario: Backup and sync are reported separately once enrolled
   ✔  Given a FileSquid desktop host enrolled with the hosted service at "backup.filesquid.tech" with backup on and sync off
   ✔  When the owner's webview reads the host descriptor with the per-launch token
   ✔  Then the hosted service should be enrolled at "backup.filesquid.tech"
   ✔  And backup should be reported on and sync off, as two separate values
  Scenario: The host type never follows the request
   ✔  Given a FileSquid desktop host that describes itself to the API
   ✔  When a caller reads the host descriptor with headers that claim a server origin and a browser user agent
   ✔  Then the descriptor should still say host type "desktop"
  Scenario: The operator name is trimmed
   ✔  Given a FileSquid server host whose operator name is set to "  Garderen Family  "
   ✔  When a signed-in member who is not an administrator reads the host descriptor
   ✔  Then the descriptor should say host type "server" with operator name "Garderen Family"
  Scenario: A descriptor change reaches every subscriber
   ✔  Given a FileSquid desktop host that describes itself to the API
   ✔  And an owner's webview and a second authenticated client subscribed to the event channel
   ✔  When the host enrols with the hosted service
   ✔  And the host then turns sync on
   ✔  Then each subscriber should receive two "host.change" events in that order
   ✔  And the first should carry the enrolled descriptor with sync off and the second the descriptor with sync on
  Scenario: A client that missed events reads the descriptor again
   ✔  Given a subscriber that received an "events.resync" hint
   ✔  When it recovers its state
   ✔  Then it should read the host descriptor through the request route
  Scenario: A caller without a credential is refused
   ✔  Given a FileSquid desktop host that describes itself to the API
   ✔  When a client reads the host descriptor without a credential
   ✔  Then it should be refused as "unauthorized" because of "missing_credentials"
   ✔  And the activity journal should record one "api.auth.denied" entry
  Scenario: The descriptor and its event carry nothing private
   ✔  Given a FileSquid desktop host enrolled with the hosted service at "backup.filesquid.tech" with backup on and sync on
   ✔  When the descriptor and a "host.change" payload are inspected
   ✔  Then neither should contain a token, content handle, key or key identifier, account or device identifier, e-mail address, quota, object count, size, timestamp, path or file name
Feature: Durable jobs are reported and controlled through the job routes
  Scenario: An interrupted job resumes without repeating completed work
   ✔> Given a host with a job service and a counting worker
   ✔  Given a counting job of 6 units that has done 3
   ✔  When I pause the job through the job routes
   ✔  And the worker reaches its next checkpoint
   ✔  Then the job should be paused with 4 units done
   ✔  When I resume the job through the job routes
   ✔  Then the job should complete
   ✔  And every unit should have been done exactly once
  Scenario: A paused job that waits does not start until it is resumed
   ✔> Given a host with a job service and a counting worker
   ✔  Given a counting job of 6 units that waits
   ✔  When I pause the job through the job routes
   ✔  Then the worker should not be able to start it
   ✔  When I resume the job through the job routes
   ✔  Then the job should complete
  Scenario: A classified kind is held to its cap
   ✔> Given a host with a job service and a counting worker
   ✔  Given transfers are capped at 1
   ✔  And two waiting transfer jobs
   ✔  When the first transfer starts
   ✔  Then the second transfer should be refused as "slots_full"
   ✔  And the second transfer should still wait
  Scenario: Every stored change of a job is announced on the event channel
   ✔> Given a host with a job service and a counting worker
   ✔  Given a counting job of 6 units that waits
   ✔  When I pause the job through the job routes
   ✔  Then its "job.update" events should show it waiting, then paused
   ✔  And no event should carry the job's source
  Scenario: A finished job refuses every control
   ✔> Given a host with a job service and a counting worker
   ✔  Given a counting job of 6 units that waits
   ✔  When I cancel the job through the job routes
   ✔  Then the job should be cancelled
   ✔  And pausing, resuming and cancelling it again should each be refused as a conflict
Feature: Library and repair routes
  Scenario: Create a library in an empty folder through the API
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's host with an empty folder registered
   ✔  When the owner creates a library in that folder through the API
   ✔  Then the answer should name an opaque library ID and no filesystem path
   ✔  And the registered folder should hold only the OCFL 1.1 storage-root declaration
  Scenario: Open a library's folder through the API
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the owner opens the library's folder through the API
   ✔  Then the answer should name the same library with 2 objects
   ✔  And the library folder should be unchanged
  Scenario: A folder that already holds a file is refused once
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's host with a registered folder that holds a file
   ✔  When the owner creates a library in that folder through the API
   ✔  Then the answer should be the error "conflict"
   ✔  And exactly one refusal "root_not_empty" should be journaled for "library.root.create"
   ✔  And the registered folder should still hold only that file
  Scenario: Copy a completed snapshot and rebuild from files through the API
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the owner copies a completed snapshot of the library to an empty folder through the API
   ✔  Then the copy should report 2 objects and no invalid object
   ✔  And the library folder should be unchanged
   ✔  When the owner rebuilds the library from its files through the API
   ✔  Then the rebuild should flag only the object without a version inventory
  Scenario: The candidate query offers only the object without a version inventory
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the owner asks the API which objects need a version-inventory repair
   ✔  Then only the object without a version inventory should be offered
  Scenario: A consented repair by the desktop owner makes the object committable
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the owner repairs the object without a version inventory with consent through the API
   ✔  Then the object should be answered as repaired
   ✔  And the consent and one outcome row per object should be recorded as "ocfl.repair.version_inventory"
   ✔  And the repaired object should accept a new version
  Scenario: A head that already has both files is refused with nothing_to_repair
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the owner repairs the complete object with consent through the API
   ✔  Then the object should be answered as refused with "nothing_to_repair"
   ✔  And the consent and one outcome row per object should be recorded as "ocfl.repair.version_inventory"
   ✔  And the object's outcome row should be failed with "nothing_to_repair"
   ✔  And the library folder should be unchanged
  Scenario: A repair without consent is refused once and changes nothing
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the owner asks the API to repair the object without a version inventory without consent
   ✔  Then the answer should be the error "conflict"
   ✔  And exactly one refusal "consent_required" should be journaled for "ocfl.repair.version_inventory"
   ✔  And the library folder should be unchanged
  Scenario: A repair without a credential is refused once
   ✔  Given the desktop owner's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When a caller without a credential asks the API to repair the object without a version inventory
   ✔  Then the answer should be the error "unauthorized"
   ✔  And exactly one refusal "missing_credentials" should be journaled for "api.auth.denied"
   ✔  And the library folder should be unchanged
  Scenario: The server administrator is refused the repair
   ✔  Given the server administrator's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the server administrator repairs the object without a version inventory with consent through the API
   ✔  Then the answer should be the error "forbidden"
   ✔  And exactly one refusal "not_permitted" should be journaled for "api.auth.denied"
   ✔  And the library folder should be unchanged
  Scenario: The server administrator can still rebuild a library
   ✔  Given the server administrator's library with a complete object and an object without a version inventory
   ✔  When the server administrator rebuilds the library from its files through the API
   ✔  Then the rebuild should flag only the object without a version inventory
Feature: Library domain commands and queries
  Scenario: Create and open a library without an account or a connection
   ✔  Given a FileSquid application layer with an empty catalog
   ✔  When the owner creates a library named "Family archive"
   ✔  Then the library should have a UUID version 4 identifier
   ✔  And opening the library should answer the name "Family archive" at revision "1"
  Scenario: Deleting a collection keeps its files
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 2 files
   ✔  When the owner deletes the collection "Trips"
   ✔  Then the library should still list 2 files
   ✔  And the unfiled listing should show 2 files
  Scenario: Removing a file from a virtual collection keeps its other memberships
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  And the file is a member of the virtual collections "Best" and "Family"
   ✔  When the owner removes the file from "Best"
   ✔  Then the collection "Trips" should still list the file
   ✔  And the virtual collection "Family" should still list the file
   ✔  And the virtual collection "Best" should list no file
  Scenario: A rule-based virtual collection refuses hand-picked members
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  And a rule-based virtual collection "Recent"
   ✔  When the owner adds the file to "Recent"
   ✔  Then the command should be refused as "invalid_request"
  Scenario: Hidden and deleted files leave the ordinary listing
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 3 files
   ✔  When the owner hides the first file and deletes the second
   ✔  Then the ordinary listing should show 1 file
   ✔  And the hidden view should show 1 file
   ✔  And the deleted view should show 1 file to be purged 30 days after deletion
  Scenario: Permanent deletion needs a confirmation of the exact scope
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 2 files
   ✔  And the owner has deleted every file
   ✔  When the owner permanently deletes every file confirming 1 file
   ✔  Then the command should be refused as "invalid_request"
   ✔  And the deleted view should show 2 files
  Scenario: A command against a stale revision is a conflict and changes nothing
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  When the owner renames the collection "Trips" with a stale revision
   ✔  Then the command should be refused as "conflict"
   ✔  And nothing should have been committed
  Scenario: Retrying a command with the same operation ID changes nothing
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  When the owner sends the same create-collection command twice
   ✔  Then both answers should name the same collection
   ✔  And the library should list 2 collections
  Scenario: A command that changes nothing stores nothing
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  When the owner renames the collection "Trips" to "Trips" as operation "op-same-title"
   ✔  Then the command should answer committed at the current revision
   ✔  And the catalog should hold no receipt for operation "op-same-title"
   ✔  And nothing should have been committed
   ✔  And no journal entry should have been stored
  Scenario: A retried command that changed nothing is planned again
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 1 file
   ✔  And the owner has renamed the collection "Trips" to "Trips" as operation "op-same-title"
   ✔  When the owner renames the collection "Trips" to "Holidays"
   ✔  And the owner retries operation "op-same-title"
   ✔  Then the command should be refused as "conflict"
  Scenario: Paging visits every file once in a stable order
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Trips" holding 7 files
   ✔  When the owner lists the library 3 files per page
   ✔  Then every file should appear exactly once in identifier order
   ✔  And a query asking for 1001 items per page should be refused
  Scenario: The journal records every change without names or titles
   ✔  Given a library with a collection "Holiday in Lisbon" holding 1 file
   ✔  When the owner renames the collection "Holiday in Lisbon" to "Secret plans"
   ✔  Then the catalog should hold one journal entry per commit
   ✔  And no journal entry should contain "Lisbon" or "Secret plans"

   ✔  And nothing should be written to the library
Feature: Metadata property routes
  Scenario: An upload creates the file's object with its original and metadata record
   ✔  When the owner uploads "Holiday 2023.JPG" as a new file
   ✔  Then the file's object should hold the original and its metadata record in version "v1"
   ✔  And the record should name "Holiday 2023.JPG" with the client's facts and the receiving host's system, architecture and FileSquid version
   ✔  And every source, volume and attribute fact the upload did not supply should be listed as unavailable
  Scenario: An upload whose declared size differs commits nothing
   ✔  When the owner uploads "a.txt" declaring 9 bytes but sending 5
   ✔  Then the metadata answer should be the error "invalid_request"
   ✔  And no file object should have been created
  Scenario: Statements are set, read and removed, one version each
   ✔  Given the owner uploaded "harbour.jpg"
   ✔  When the owner sets "dcterms:title" to "Harbour"
   ✔  And the owner sets "dc:creator" to "Ana"
   ✔  And the owner removes "dcterms:creator"
   ✔  Then the file should show only "dcterms:title" as "Harbour"
   ✔  And the file's object should be at version "v4"
  Scenario: Unknown Dublin Core names and unregistered prefixes are refused
   ✔  Given the owner uploaded "a.txt"
   ✔  When the owner sets "dcterms:colour" to "red"
   ✔  Then the metadata answer should be the error "invalid_request"
   ✔  When the owner sets "rico:title" to "Fonds"
   ✔  Then the metadata answer should be the error "invalid_request"
   ✔  And the file's object should be at version "v1"
  Scenario: A registered namespace accepts its own terms
   ✔  Given the owner uploaded "a.txt"
   ✔  When the owner registers the namespace "rico" with the term "title"
   ✔  And the owner sets "rico:title" to "Fonds"
   ✔  Then the file should show only "rico:title" as "Fonds"
  Scenario: A bare term name is offered Dublin Core first
   ✔  Given the owner registered the namespace "rico" with the term "title"
   ✔  When the owner asks where "title" can go
   ✔  Then the answer should offer "dcterms:title", "schema:title" and "rico:title" in that order
  Scenario: The import record's facts show no absolute path, host or account
   ✔  Given the owner uploaded "notes.txt"
   ✔  When the owner reads the file's facts
   ✔  Then the facts should name "notes.txt" with its size and method "api_upload"
   ✔  And the facts should hold no absolute path, import root, host or account name
  Scenario: Content answers use the record's display name and media type
   ✔  Given the owner uploaded "Café.md" as "text/markdown"
   ✔  When the owner reads the file's original
   ✔  Then the answer should carry the media type "text/markdown" and the name "Café.md"
