  Scenario: A stray file under vN/ degrades health until it is removed
   ✔  Given an open library with a valid object and an object with a stray file under its head version
   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  Then health should be "degraded" with reason "ocfl_validation_errors" naming "E023" in 1 object
   ✔  And one "ocfl.validation.finding" entry for "E023" should be journaled without paths
   ✔  When the stray file is removed and the library is validated again
   ✔  Then one "ocfl.validation.finding_closed" entry for "E023" should be journaled without paths
   ✔  And health should be "ok"
  Scenario: An object without a version inventory is a warning only
   ✔  Given an open library with an object whose head has no version inventory
   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  Then the findings should list "W010" as a warning
   ✔  And one "ocfl.validation.finding" entry for "W010" should be journaled without paths
   ✔  And health should be "ok"
  Scenario: The takeover check validates the object a takeover commit wrote

   ✔  When the takeover check runs for the object with the stray file after a commit that took over an expired reservation
   ✔  Then health should be "degraded" with reason "ocfl_validation_errors" naming "E023" in 1 object
  Scenario: The administrator sees findings without locations

   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  Then the owner should see where the "E023" finding is
   ✔  And the server administrator should see the "E023" finding without its location
  Scenario: A capped run reports what it did not check and the next run resumes

   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  Then the run should be "capped" with 2 objects checked and 1 not checked
   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  Then the cursor should have wrapped to the first object
  Scenario: Deleting an object closes its findings

   ✔  When the owner requests a validation run
   ✔  And the object with the stray file is deleted and the library is validated again
   ✔  Then one "ocfl.validation.finding_closed" entry for "E023" should be journaled without paths
   ✔  And health should be "ok"

Feature: PROV-O records, history routes and backfill in filesquid-api
  Scenario: A repaired object gets a provenance record under the repair run ID
   ✔  Given a library with an object whose head has no version inventory, recording provenance
   ✔  When the owner repairs that object
   ✔  Then a new version of the object should hold a PROV-O record of kind "repair"
   ✔  And the record, the consent and the outcome should share the repair run ID
   ✔  And the new version should carry its "ocfl.commit" journal entry under the ID the journal stored
  Scenario: Without a provenance context the repair says that no record was written
   ✔  Given a library with an object whose head has no version inventory, without provenance
   ✔  When the owner repairs that object
   ✔  Then the repair answer should name the provenance error "provenance_not_configured"
   ✔  And a "prov.record" warning should be journaled under the repair run ID
  Scenario: A backfill reconstructs every missing record once
   ✔  Given a library of 2 objects committed before the history existed
   ✔  When the history backfill runs
   ✔  Then every version should have a record and the earlier ones should be marked reconstructed
   ✔  And the backfill process should end "completed" with 2 of 2 objects done
   ✔  When the history backfill runs again
   ✔  Then the second run should commit nothing
  Scenario: A backfill stops at its cap and the next run continues
   ✔  Given a library of 2 objects committed before the history existed
   ✔  When the history backfill runs with a cap of 3 records
   ✔  Then the run should report that it reached its cap
   ✔  When the history backfill runs again
   ✔  Then every version should have a record and the earlier ones should be marked reconstructed
  Scenario: The history of a file lists its activities in commit order
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller lists the history of the first file
   ✔  Then the answer should hold 2 entries of that file, oldest first
  Scenario: The history is paged with an opaque cursor
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller lists the whole history 2 entries at a time
   ✔  Then the pages should hold 2 and then 1 entries and the last page no cursor
  Scenario: The record of one version is raw JSON-LD
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller reads the record of version "v2" of the first file
   ✔  Then the answer should be the JSON-LD record of that version
  Scenario: A version without a record is not found
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller reads the record of version "v9" of the first file
   ✔  Then the response should be an "not_found" error with a message
  Scenario: Two filter dimensions at once are refused
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller lists the history by object and by operation at once
   ✔  Then the response should be an "invalid_request" error with a message
  Scenario: A redacted export names no installation and no path
   ✔  Given a history holding 2 versions of one file and 1 version of another
   ✔  When the caller exports the history of the first file redacted
   ✔  Then the export should be a JSON-LD array of 2 records without the installation or any path
  Scenario: The history routes wait for the host to install its history
   ✔  Given a host that has not installed its history yet
   ✔  When the caller lists the history of the first file
   ✔  Then the response should be an "unavailable" error with a message
   ✔  And the error should be retryable
Feature: Replica, cache identity and pending changes
  Scenario: A stored view answers with its coverage
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given the replica stored a complete view for alice
   ✔  When alice reads the view from the replica
   ✔  Then the answer should say the coverage is complete
  Scenario: An incomplete view says so
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given the replica stored a view with 3 of 8 index parts loaded
   ✔  When alice reads the view from the replica
   ✔  Then the answer should say 3 of 8 parts are loaded
  Scenario: Another viewer never reads alice's stored view
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given the replica stored a complete view for alice
   ✔  When bob reads the same view from the replica
   ✔  Then the replica should report "not_cached"

   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"

   ✔  When alice's grants shrink to "file.read" and she reads the same view
   ✔  Then the replica should report "not_cached"

   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  Given the replica holds a view made for bob under alice's key
   ✔  When alice reads the view from the replica
   ✔  Then the replica should report "identity_mismatch"
  Scenario: An evicted view says it was evicted
   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"

   ✔  When alice reads the view from the replica
   ✔  Then the replica should report "evicted"
   ✔  And the answer should not be an empty page

   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"
   ✔  When alice hides a file while offline
   ✔  Then the change should be "queued"
   ✔  And library "family" should have 1 pending change

   ✔> Given a replica for viewer "alice" on library "family" with grants "file.read" and "file.write"

   ✔  When the change begins publishing and the head publication succeeds at revision "4"

   ✔  And library "family" should have 0 pending changes

   ✔  And the second change should not be allowed to publish

Feature: Reusable filesquid-metadata engine
  Scenario: Core API is namespaced metadata
fsquid metadata — metadata CRUD (via filesquid-ocfl)

Usage:
  fsquid <command> [args] [--object <dir>] [--id <object-id>]
  fsquid metadata <command> [args] [--object <dir>] [--id <object-id>]

Commands:
  extract <file>          Extract EXIF/ID3/XMP from a media file into metadata.md
  extract --file <logical-path>
                          Extract from a logical file of the object
  get [prefix:term]       Print populated statements (or one CURIE)
  set <prefix:term> <val> Set a statement and commit metadata.md
  export csv|xmp|json <path>  Write CSV, XMP or JSON sidecar interchange
  import csv|xmp|json <path>  Read CSV, XMP or JSON sidecar into metadata.md
  validate [prefix:term]  Check a term against the /terms/ register and the
                          namespace registry, or, without a term, every
                          statement of metadata.md; names each unregistered
                          prefix (always run as `fsquid metadata validate`)
  namespace register <prefix> <iri> [--terms a,b,...] [--custom]
                          Register an extension namespace (optional term
                          list) or, with --custom, declare a custom namespace
                          of free keys
  namespace list          Print the registered namespaces
  help                    Show this help

--object is an OCFL object directory (default `.`). --id names the object:
required to create one in a directory without an object, and checked
against an existing object's identifier.

--registry is the OCFL object directory that keeps the namespace registry
(namespaces.md); without it, the registry is the one in --object.
dcterms:, dc:, schema: and exif: are built in and cannot be registered.

JSON sidecars also take --file <logical-path>: the logical file of the
object's head whose SHA-256 digest binds the sidecar with the object
identifier. Import rejects a sidecar whose identifier or digest differs.

Canonical store: metadata.md (YAML front matter + Markdown body), a logical
file of the OCFL object. Each edit commits a new OCFL version; earlier
versions are never modified.
   ✔  When I inspect the filesquid-metadata public API

Feature: PROV-O model and profile filesquid-prov/1
  Scenario: One activity describes the committed version
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then the record should hold exactly one prov:Activity named after the operation, object and version
   ✔  And its end time should equal the version's created time
   ✔  And it should name the journal entry IDs of the commit
  Scenario: Added, changed, moved and removed paths become entities
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then the changed path should be generated by the activity and derived from its previous state
   ✔  And the moved path should be derived from the old path's previous state
   ✔  And the removed path's previous state should be invalidated by the activity
   ✔  And every previous state the activity replaced should be used by it
   ✔  And every entity should carry its object ID, version, logical path and SHA-256 digest
  Scenario: The owner is an installation agent, never a person
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then the activity should be associated with "urn:uuid:" followed by the installation UUID
   ✔  And the FileSquid host should be a prov:SoftwareAgent acting on behalf of the installation
   ✔  And the record should contain no prov:Person and no display name
  Scenario: Agents the caller did not observe are stated as unrecorded
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given the caller observed no installation and no host
   ✔  When I build its PROV-O record
   ✔  Then both agents should be stated as unrecorded
  Scenario: Equal facts give equal bytes
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I serialize the record twice from equal facts
   ✔  Then both documents should be byte-for-byte equal
   ✔  And the graph should list the activity first, then agents and entities sorted by identifier
  Scenario: A record survives a parse round trip
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I serialize the record and parse it again
   ✔  Then the parsed record should equal the original
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that is larger than 1 MiB
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_TOO_LARGE"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that nests deeper than 16 levels
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_TOO_DEEP"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that repeats a key
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_DUPLICATE_KEY"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that names an unknown profile
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_UNKNOWN_PROFILE"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario Outline: Parsing refuses a record that breaks a bound
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given a stored record that holds an entity with a forged ID
   ✔  When I parse it
   ✔  Then parsing should fail with "PROV_INVALID_NODE"
   ✔  And the error message should name no stored value
  Scenario: The history index pages an object's activities in order
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given an index of 5 activities across 2 objects
   ✔  When I read the activities of the first object in pages of 2
   ✔  Then I should read 3 activities ordered by end time then activity ID
   ✔  And the last page should carry no cursor
  Scenario Outline: Page sizes outside 1 to 1000 are refused
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given an index of 5 activities across 2 objects
   ✔  When I ask for a page of 0 activities
   ✔  Then the page request should be refused
  Scenario Outline: Page sizes outside 1 to 1000 are refused
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given an index of 5 activities across 2 objects
   ✔  When I ask for a page of 1 activities
   ✔  Then the page request should be accepted
  Scenario Outline: Page sizes outside 1 to 1000 are refused
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given an index of 5 activities across 2 objects
   ✔  When I ask for a page of 1000 activities
   ✔  Then the page request should be accepted
  Scenario Outline: Page sizes outside 1 to 1000 are refused
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  Given an index of 5 activities across 2 objects
   ✔  When I ask for a page of 1001 activities
   ✔  Then the page request should be refused
  Scenario: The module stands alone
   ✔> Given the facts of version "v3" of an object whose base version is "v2"
   ✔  When I inspect the crate manifest
   ✔  Then no default dependency should be another FileSquid crate
   ✔  And no feature should name filesquid-native or filesquid-c2pa

   ✔  Then the build should succeed

Feature: Desktop capabilities are deny-by-default
  Scenario: The shipped capability set passes the deny-by-default policy
   ✔> Given the desktop host's shipped capability set
   ✔  Then the capability set should have no policy violations
  Scenario: The main window may listen for FileSquid events and nothing else
   ✔> Given the desktop host's shipped capability set
   ✔  Then the main window should be granted exactly "core:event:allow-listen" and "core:event:allow-unlisten"
   ✔  And no Tauri command of the app should be defined
  Scenario Outline: A capability that grants file, SQL, program or dialog access is refused
   ✔> Given the desktop host's shipped capability set
   ✔  When a capability grants "fs:default" to the main window
   ✔  Then the policy should refuse it as the forbidden plugin permission "fs:default"
  Scenario Outline: A capability that grants file, SQL, program or dialog access is refused
   ✔> Given the desktop host's shipped capability set
   ✔  When a capability grants "sql:default" to the main window
   ✔  Then the policy should refuse it as the forbidden plugin permission "sql:default"
  Scenario Outline: A capability that grants file, SQL, program or dialog access is refused
   ✔> Given the desktop host's shipped capability set
   ✔  When a capability grants "shell:allow-execute" to the main window
   ✔  Then the policy should refuse it as the forbidden plugin permission "shell:allow-execute"
  Scenario Outline: A capability that grants file, SQL, program or dialog access is refused
   ✔> Given the desktop host's shipped capability set
   ✔  When a capability grants "dialog:allow-open" to the main window
   ✔  Then the policy should refuse it as the forbidden plugin permission "dialog:allow-open"
  Scenario: A capability that would hand the window filesystem paths is refused
   ✔> Given the desktop host's shipped capability set
   ✔  When a capability grants "core:path:default" to the main window
   ✔  Then the policy should refuse it as the ungranted permission "core:path:default"
  Scenario: A capability for every window is refused
   ✔> Given the desktop host's shipped capability set
   ✔  When a capability names the window "*"
   ✔  Then the policy should refuse it as the unknown window "*"
  Scenario: A capability for remote pages is refused
   ✔> Given the desktop host's shipped capability set
   ✔  When a capability grants event listening to remote pages
   ✔  Then the policy should refuse it for its remote context
  Scenario: A Tauri command defined next to the handler in lib.rs is refused
   ✔> Given the desktop host's shipped capability set
   ✔  When lib.rs defines the Tauri command "health_ping"
   ✔  Then the policy should refuse the command "health_ping" in lib.rs
  Scenario: A Tauri command that no capability authorizes is refused
   ✔> Given the desktop host's shipped capability set
   ✔  When a submodule defines the Tauri command "read_any_file"
   ✔  Then the policy should refuse the unlisted command "read_any_file"
